# CLOSING PASS 3 -- `crimewidthkill` (#529): is the regression test complete now?  Liveness token `CRIME_CLOSE3_20260913`.

VERDICT LINE FIRST: `CLOSES` (no floor gap remains in the 121-row test) or `GAP: <exact source edit to dev_sgi_ip32.c>`,
then the token.

This is a routine software-testing review of an open-source hardware emulator (GXemul) and its regression test.
"Mutant" means a deliberately modified copy of the emulator's C source used to check that the test notices the change
(standard mutation testing).  Please check the premises, not only the reasoning, and say what you could not check.
Tree: `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\` only.  File-reading seats: `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c`
(`DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`), `regress/crime_width_probe.py`, `regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/`.

## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO

The second closing pass found gaps in the 111-row test and a measuring seat found one more in the repair; the test was
repaired twice (repair 6: 121 rows; repair 7: the IRQ registers' seed).  Several of those findings were members of
CLASSES that earlier passes had also hit (answers at labels; the latch keyed on the access; re-arm / pre-set by an
accepted access; effects keyed on the address).  This project's rule for that: when two consecutive passes find breaches
of one class, the repair must close the WHOLE CLASS and the contract must say which cells are covered -- and the next
pass VERIFIES THE ENUMERATION rather than hunting single cells.  So this pass is asked, first: is each class's
enumeration (in the probe header's CONTRACT and in POSTTABLE 5's DENOMINATOR) complete and true?  The chain closes on
the first valid pass that finds zero floor breaches; review effort is scoped to the repair diff below, the floor question
to the whole test.

## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)

* **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of five passes found it correct): `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` used to stop the
  emulator on any access of length other than 8 or 4.  It now prints one latched `(once)` notice per device instance
  (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common tail, which answers
  a read 0 and returns 1.
* **The test's history**: 40 rows -> 71 -> 85 -> 111 -> 121 (repairs 6 and 7), SIX mutation-test tables, each with a
  written PRETABLE before it ran; tables 4-6 are 57/57, 59/60 and 27/27 predicted red-row sets exact (the one miss is
  explained in POSTTABLE 5 and was re-run alone).
* **A refused read's answer** is the low `len` bytes of whatever `odata` the decline hands the tail (memory_writemax64),
  sign- or zero-extended by the instruction.  A register-answering mutant is visible only where the register's bytes
  that reach the guest are nonzero: the whole-register form hands over the LOW bytes; the lane form
  (`reg >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))`) hands over the bytes the ADDRESS names -- bytes 0-1 at a label.
* **DISCLOSED coverage debt** (the probe header lists it): interior accept slots, an effect keyed on ONE unnamed offset,
  a store landing only at CRIME_TIME (<= 0xffff), joint multi-axis predicates, a function-static latch, widths 3/5/6/7,
  a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch (it stops the CPUs), and the lane-form answer at REV / CONTROL / TIME
  (the model holds those bytes at 0).  A finding there is not a gap unless you show the disclosure is wrong.

## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
{
	/*
	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
	 *  physical 0x14000000.
	 *
	 *  On my real O2:
	 *
	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *
	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
	 *  would work too.)
	 *
	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
	 */
	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
	size_t i;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  Set crime version/revision:
	 *
	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
	 *
	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
	 */
	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;

	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	/*
	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
	 *
	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
	 *  bank #0."
	 */
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int mb_per_bank;
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
		mb_per_bank = 32;
	else
		mb_per_bank = 128;

	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
	size_t total_mb = 0;
	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
		else
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;

		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
	}
	
	if (len == 8) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
		else
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
	} else if (len == 4) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			if (relative_addr & 4) {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
			} else {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
			}
		} else {
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
			if (relative_addr & 4)
				odata = (int32_t)odata;
			else
				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
		}
	} else {
		/*
		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
		 *
		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
		 */
		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
		}
		goto crime_declined;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

 crime_declined:
	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
	    and is still answered.  */
	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```

     The INTSTAT/INTMASK/TIME rows came from the pass-2 Opus seat, which MEASURED a decline narrowed to skip those

     *** THE sh08 AND sw08 CHANNELS EXIST BECAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT, `crimectllane' (filed): the switch matches
     CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at
     +0x08, carries bit 11 into HARD_RESET, while NetBSD's +0x0c write does nothing.  A round that fixes that lane
     removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***
  4. the latch removed, never set, or KEYED ON THE ACCESS -- the class closed on its single axes after two passes
     found members (F2: `|= len', `|= relative_addr', `<< writeflag'; closing pass: keyed on CRIME_CONTROL, on
     `relative_addr >= 0x40', on address parity).  A keyed latch shows only when the first refusal in a session falls
     where it does not set, so every axis value is a FIRST refusal followed by another, with an announce count:
        width 1 or 2 and direction R or W  warm once (sb, write, odd), latch x5 (lh, read, even)
        each label first                   rev once, sh08b once (CONTROL), istat once, imask once, dog once,
                                           time once, latch x5 (default-arm, low), hi once (the far block,

     and every label-first session refuses again at ANOTHER label (codex, second closing pass: a latch whose bit is

     and the latch PRE-SET before any refusal, in EVERY switch arm an accepted access reaches first in some counted

DISCLOSED, not rowed:
  * `goto crime_declined' -> `return 1' is EQUIVALENT on every channel: memory_rw pre-zeroes a device read buffer
    (#95, src/cpus/memory_rw.c -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)' then `memset(data, 0, len);', immediately above
    `paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr;'), and on a declined access nothing between the entry and the tail writes
    HARDINT or SOFTINT, so the tail's restore and recompute are identities.  Measured 40/40 (the then 40-row test) in kill table 1.
  * a fall-through confined to the two label cells whose arms have NO body for that access, both EQUIVALENT:
      CRIME_DOG (0x30)       `No warning.' -- the arm is `break;'
      CRIME_CONTROL, reads   the arm acts only on MEM_WRITE
    Each measured as a survivor in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through).

    the window (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0x100', kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.

    label) -- a refused store that lands only at

    +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
    offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
    keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
    kind (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8
    carried by no refusal at all).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
    OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
    which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
    (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
  * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
    every access, srelude rewrites REV = 0x11
    and CONTROL = 0x100 on every access; TIME is kept to 32 bits).  At INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG the seeds make those
    lanes nonzero, so the same edit shows (Opus, second closing pass).
  * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
    default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
  * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
    CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
  * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
    (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
  * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
    process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
    recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
  * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
    not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
  * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).

```

+x, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x1fc, 0xffff); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x1f8, "a0"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x1f8, 0x0123456789abcdef); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x1ff, "t1"); c += x
+t, clean = session(c + ["print v0", "print a1", "print a0"] + roundtrip(A))
+mid_dflt = (dflt(t, clean, 0x1ff, False) + dflt(t, clean, 0x1fe, False) + dflt(t, clean, 0x1ff, True) +
+            dflt(t, clean, 0x1fc, True)) if clean else "unclean"
+row("mid said", said(t, 1, 0x1ff, clean), 1, "lb +0x1ff (bits 0-8 set): own announce, once")
+row("mid nodflt", mid_dflt, 0, "switch default arm NOT reached at 0x1ff/0x1fe/0x1fc")
+row("mid lb v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb +0x1ff over a seeded slot answers 0")
+row("mid lh v0", pr(t, "a1"), "0x0", "refused lh +0x1fe over a seeded slot answers 0")
+row("mid keeps", pr(t, "a0"), "0x123456789abcdef", "refused sb +0x1ff, sh +0x1fc do not land (ld +0x1f8)")
+row("mid once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "5 refusals with an accepted sd between: one notice")
+row("mid surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "the session completes (a RAM trip)")
+
 # ---- the latch, first refusal at CRIME_DOG (the one named label with no session whose FIRST refusal is there) ----
+#  CRIME_DOG is SEEDED first (kimi CLI, second closing pass: with DOG at 0 a decline answering the register read 0), and
+#  read refused at BOTH widths (codex: `odata = 0x100' at DOG left the lb's low byte 0 and passed).
 A = Addr(CODE)
+s, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x30, 0x0123456789abcdef)
 x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x30, "v0")
 y, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v1")
 z, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x08, "a0")        # a refused read at CRIME_CONTROL (codex, closing pass: answers at labels)
-t, clean = session(x + y + z + ["print v0", "print a0"])
+w, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x30, "t1")
+t, clean = session(s + x + y + z + w + ["print v0", "print a0", "print t1"])
 row("dog once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "first refusal at CRIME_DOG, then +0x07: one notice")
-row("dog v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb at CRIME_DOG answers 0")
-row("ctl v0", pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused lh at CRIME_CONTROL answers 0")
+row("dog v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb at CRIME_DOG (seeded 0x0123..ef) answers 0")
+row("dog lh v0", pr(t, "t1"), "0x0", "refused lh at CRIME_DOG (seeded) answers 0")
+row("ctl v0", pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused lh at CRIME_CONTROL (holds 0x100) answers 0")
 
 # ---- FREE-RUNNING: the announce is a fatal(), so it prints when the guest is NOT single-stepped (a debug() would not) ----
@@ -654,4 +753,8 @@ for op, off, val in (("sw", 0x08, 0x01), ("sw", 0x34, 0x0badcafe)):
     x, _ = st(A, op, CRIME + off, val); c += x
 x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x34, "a0"); c += x
+#  and an accepted read of CRIME_REV (glm, second closing pass: no counted session reached the REV arm before its first
+#  refusal, so a pre-set there passed).  The other arms are reached first elsewhere: CONTROL here, INTMASK in the IRQ
+#  sessions' setup, DOG in warm once and the dog session, TIME in time once, the default arm here and in the top session.
+x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x00, "a1"); c += x
 #  lh FIRST (Opus seat, F2): a latch whose bit is taken from the access -- `d->warned |= len' or `|= relative_addr' --
 #  sets bit 0 only for a 1-byte access or an odd address, so the lh at +0x06 would leave it clear and the lb after it
```

## THE MUTANTS OF TABLES 5 AND 6, verbatim (so each named mutant's EDIT is visible, not just its name)

Each entry is (find-text, replacement) on `dev_sgi_ip32.c`; `g(...)` puts its argument in place of the decline arm's
`goto crime_declined;`.

```python
M.update({
    # codex: the latch bit keyed on ONE label (the define is used by both the test and the set)
    "c2_lbl_imask": [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK))")],
    "c2_lbl_istat": [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT))")],
    "c2_lbl_ctl":   [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL))")],
    # codex: a halfword-only nonzero answer at DOG; kimi CLI: a decline answering THE REGISTER at a label
    "c2_dog100":    [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_DOG) odata = 0x100; goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_reg_imask": [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_reg_istat": [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_reg_dog":   [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_DOG) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
    # kimi CLI: a landing that ORs into INTMASK
    "c2_land_or":   [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK) "
                              "d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / 8] |= idata; goto crime_declined;"))],
    # codex: re-arm inside CONTROL's DOG_ENABLE branch; and the same inside the other branches an accepted access takes
    "c2_rearm_dog": [(DOGBR, DOGBR + "\t\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n")],
    "c2_rearm_txt": [(TXTBR, "\t\t\tif (idata != 0 && ((d->warned = 0) == 0))\n\t\t\t\tfatal(\"[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented \"\n")],
    "c2_rearm_rev": [(REVIF, REVIF + "\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n")],
    # glm: a pre-set in a switch arm -- the INTMASK/INTSTAT arm (PREDICTED DETECTED) and the REV arm (the open member)
    "c2_pre_irq":   [(IRQARM, IRQARM.replace("\t\tcrime_interrupt_reassert(d);\n",
                                             "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n\t\tcrime_interrupt_reassert(d);\n"))],
    "c2_pre_rev":   [(REVIF, "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + REVIF)],
    # kimi-k3:cloud: decline effects keyed on an ADDRESS BIT no refusal carried (bit 7, bit 8)
    "c2_bit7_ans":  [(GOTO, g("odata = relative_addr & 0x80; goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_bit8_land": [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && (relative_addr & 0x100)) d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata; "
                              "goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_bit7_quiet": [(B.LATCH_TEST, "\t\tif (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN) && !(relative_addr & 0x80)) {")],
    "c2_bit8_ft":   [(GOTO, g("if (!(relative_addr & 0x100)) goto crime_declined;"))],
    "c2_bit8_rearm": [(ARM8, ARM8.replace("\tif (len == 8) {\n", "\tif (len == 8) {\n\t\tif (relative_addr & 0x100) d->warned = 0;\n", 1))],
    "c2_bit8_latch": [(B.LATCH_SET, "\t\t\td->warned |= (relative_addr & 0x100) ? 0 : CRIME_W_LEN;\n")],
})

LANE = "odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7))); goto crime_declined;"

M = dict(E.M)
_p = os.path.join(HERE, "mutants_close2_opus_b3.py")
_ns = {}
exec(io.open(_p, encoding="utf-8").read().split("name, path = sys.argv")[0], _ns)
M.update({"q_" + k: v for k, v in _ns["M"].items() if k != "base"})


def lane(label):
    return [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_READ && relative_addr == %s) %s" % (label, LANE)))]


M.update({
    # the Opus seat's istat_lane, at the other labels: INTMASK and DOG are seeded and should show;
    # REV, CONTROL and TIME hold 0 in the lanes a label's refused read names -- PREDICTED EQUIVALENT, disclosed
    "l_imask": lane("CRIME_INTMASK"),
    "l_dog":   lane("CRIME_DOG"),
    "l_rev":   lane("CRIME_REV"),
    "l_ctl":   lane("CRIME_CONTROL"),
    "l_time":  lane("CRIME_TIME"),
})

```

## QUESTIONS

1. **Verify the enumeration.**  For each class the CONTRACT claims closed -- the answer at every named label, the latch
   keyed on any single axis of the access, pre-set and re-arm by an accepted access in every arm and branch, effects
   keyed on any single address bit, refused stores that land -- is the enumeration complete and TRUE of the rows?  Name
   any cell the enumeration claims that no row can see, or any cell of the class it omits.
2. **Is there a floor gap in the 121-row test?**  The smallest source edit to `dev_sgi_ip32.c` that undoes part of the
   fix's behaviour and still scores 121/121, as an exact edit, with the row you expected to catch it and why it cannot.
3. **Which statement in this brief, in POSTTABLE 5 or 6, or in the probe header, is wrong?**


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  12: ## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO
  13: 
  14: The second closing pass found gaps in the 111-row test and a measuring seat found one more in the repair; the test was
  15: repaired twice (repair 6: 121 rows; repair 7: the IRQ registers' seed).  Several of those findings were members of
  16: CLASSES that earlier passes had also hit (answers at labels; the latch keyed on the access; re-arm / pre-set by an
  17: accepted access; effects keyed on the address).  This project's rule for that: when two consecutive passes find breaches
  18: of one class, the repair must close the WHOLE CLASS and the contract must say which cells are covered -- and the next
  19: pass VERIFIES THE ENUMERATION rather than hunting single cells.  So this pass is asked, first: is each class's
  20: enumeration (in the probe header's CONTRACT and in POSTTABLE 5's DENOMINATOR) complete and true?  The chain closes on
  21: the first valid pass that finds zero floor breaches; review effort is scoped to the repair diff below, the floor question
  22: to the whole test.
  23: 
  24: ## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)
  25: 
  26: * **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of five passes found it correct): `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` used to stop the
  27:   emulator on any access of length other than 8 or 4.  It now prints one latched `(once)` notice per device instance
  28:   (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common tail, which answers
  29:   a read 0 and returns 1.
  30: * **The test's history**: 40 rows -> 71 -> 85 -> 111 -> 121 (repairs 6 and 7), SIX mutation-test tables, each with a
  31:   written PRETABLE before it ran; tables 4-6 are 57/57, 59/60 and 27/27 predicted red-row sets exact (the one miss is
  32:   explained in POSTTABLE 5 and was re-run alone).
  33: * **A refused read's answer** is the low `len` bytes of whatever `odata` the decline hands the tail (memory_writemax64),
  34:   sign- or zero-extended by the instruction.  A register-answering mutant is visible only where the register's bytes
  35:   that reach the guest are nonzero: the whole-register form hands over the LOW bytes; the lane form
  36:   (`reg >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))`) hands over the bytes the ADDRESS names -- bytes 0-1 at a label.
  37: * **DISCLOSED coverage debt** (the probe header lists it): interior accept slots, an effect keyed on ONE unnamed offset,
  38:   a store landing only at CRIME_TIME (<= 0xffff), joint multi-axis predicates, a function-static latch, widths 3/5/6/7,
  39:   a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch (it stops the CPUs), and the lane-form answer at REV / CONTROL / TIME
  40:   (the model holds those bytes at 0).  A finding there is not a gap unless you show the disclosure is wrong.
  41: 
  42: ## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)
  43: 
  44: ```c
  45: DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)

  48: 	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
  49: 	 *  physical 0x14000000.

  51: 	 *  On my real O2:

  53: 	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  54: 	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  55: 	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  56: 	 *
  57: 	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
  58: 	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
  59: 	 *  would work too.)
  60: 	 *
  61: 	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
  62: 	 */
  63: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
  64: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  65: 	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];

  74: 	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it

  76: 	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.

  78: 	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess

 152: 		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
 153: 		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
 154: 		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.

 156: 		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the

 158: 		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
 159: 		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
 160: 		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
 161: 		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not

 180: 		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.

 182: 		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,

 190: 		 *  4.18 works too.

 192: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
 193: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
 194: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
 195: 		 */

 200: 			else
 201: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 202: 		}
 203: 		break;

 208: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 209: 			/*

 214: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
 215: 			}
 216: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {

 236: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
 237: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);

 241: 		// No warning.
 242: 		break;

 244: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */

 248: 	default:

 264: 	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
 265: 	    and is still answered.  */
 266: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
 267: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 268: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 269: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 270: 
 271: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 272: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 273: 
 274: 	return 1;
 275: }
 276: ```
 277: 
 278: ## THE TEST'S HEADER -- CONTRACT and DISCLOSED, verbatim
 279: 
 280: ```
 281: CONTRACT -- the edits this detector is designed to catch (clause (e)), each MEASURED in a kill table under
 282: artifacts/crimewidthkill/ (killtable_20260913/ through killtable6_20260913/):
 283:   1. the kill reinstated verbatim, or any other host death at the width arm, before or after the announce
 284:                                                        <op> surv (six widths), hi surv, latch surv, sb08 run
 285:   2. FAULT: the width arm returning 0 (a guest data bus error, EXCEPTION_DBE)   lb/lh/sb/sh pc and exc, lb/lh/lbu/lhu v0
 286:   3. the refused access FALLING INTO THE REGISTER SWITCH -- the goto lost, or narrowed by width, direction, or ADDRESS.
 287:      The switch's observable arms, per refusal cell:
 288:         CRIME_CONTROL (+0x08), writes     sb 0x01 prints the unimplemented-control fatal    sb08 msg
 289:                                           sh 0x0800 stops every CPU (HARD_RESET)            sh08 run, sh08 pc
 290:                                           and the arm's three TESTED idata bits (DOG 0x200, RESET 0x800,
 291:                                           ENDIANESS 0x100) each both ways at 2W: sh 0x0201,
 292:                                           0x0900, 0x0a00 with sh08 0x0800       sh08b msg, sh08b run
 293:                                           plus ALL-ONES refused writes, sb 0xff and sh 0xffff (kimi CLI,
 294:                                           closing pass), so a fall-through keyed on any bit a 1- or 2-byte
 295:                                           store can carry shows (bits above 15 never reach a refused access,
 296:                                           so a conjunct on them is equivalent)  sb08 msg, sh08b msg/run
 297:         and the refused stores at INTSTAT, INTMASK and TIME carry all-ones (0xff / 0xffff) for the same reason
 298:         CRIME_REV (+0x00), reads AND writes, from pc 0xbfc05180: the PROM-probe hack sets pc = ra (it does not test
 299:                                           the direction)                                    lb/lh/sb/sh rev pc
 300:         CRIME_INTMASK (+0x18), CRIME_INTSTAT (+0x10), all four cells: the arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(),
 301:                                           which raises cause.IP2 after a SOFTINT store   imask/istat lb, sb, lh, sh
 302:         CRIME_TIME (+0x38), all four cells: the arm advances TIME, which the NEXT read shows   time lb, sb, lh, sh
 303:         the default arm (any unlabelled offset): a debug() line, printed while stepping      lb/lh/sb/sh nodflt,
 304:                                           at +0x07, +0x06, +0x1f, +0x1e and +0x27f           hi nodflt
 305:      Each channel carries a LIVE control on an accepted width that shows it can vary:
 306:         rev pc ctl (lw redirects), sw08 halts (sw 0x800 stops the CPUs), sw08 msg ctl (sw 0x01 prints the text),
 307:         imask/istat pre + ctl (IP2 low after the setup; an accepted lw sets it), time lb/sb/lh/sh ctl (an accepted ld
 308:         moves TIME), dflt ctl (lw +0x04 prints the default-arm line).
 309:      The INTSTAT/INTMASK/TIME rows came from the pass-2 Opus seat, which MEASURED a decline narrowed to skip those
 310:      labels passing the 40-row version of this detector 40/40 (opus_pass2_20260913/OPUS_REPORT.md); their 2-byte cells
 311:      from the F2 pass (grok, glm): `len != 2 || relative_addr != CRIME_INTSTAT' passed the 71-row version.  So every
 312:      observable label carries the whole {1,2} x {R,W} refusal floor (CONTROL's reads and DOG have no body: below).
 313:      *** THE sh08 AND sw08 CHANNELS EXIST BECAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT, `crimectllane' (filed): the switch matches
 314:      CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at
 315:      +0x08, carries bit 11 into HARD_RESET, while NetBSD's +0x0c write does nothing.  A round that fixes that lane
 316:      removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***
 317:   4. the latch removed, never set, or KEYED ON THE ACCESS -- the class closed on its single axes after two passes
 318:      found members (F2: `|= len', `|= relative_addr', `<< writeflag'; closing pass: keyed on CRIME_CONTROL, on
 319:      `relative_addr >= 0x40', on address parity).  A keyed latch shows only when the first refusal in a session falls
 320:      where it does not set, so every axis value is a FIRST refusal followed by another, with an announce count:
 321:         width 1 or 2 and direction R or W  warm once (sb, write, odd), latch x5 (lh, read, even)
 322:         each label first                   rev once, sh08b once (CONTROL), istat once, imask once, dog once,
 323:                                            time once, latch x5 (default-arm, low), hi once (the far block,
 324:                                            followed by refusals there and one below 0x40), mid once
 325:      and every label-first session refuses again at ANOTHER label (codex, second closing pass: a latch whose bit is
 326:      keyed on one label, `CRIME_W_LEN << (relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK)', passed the 111-row version: the imask,
 327:      istat and sh08b sessions refused only at their own label)
 328:      and the latch PRE-SET before any refusal, in EVERY switch arm an accepted access reaches first in some counted
 329:      session: by an accepted 4-byte access, by a second latch on the CONTROL text reusing bit 0 (Opus closing seat:
 330:      `pre4', `ctl_collide'), and in the REV arm (glm, second closing pass) -- latch x5's prologue; by an 8-byte one,
 331:      warm once (sd first) and time once (ld first); in the INTMASK arm, the IRQ sessions' setup; DOG, the dog session
 332:   5. the announce: its text (`unimplemented len=N at 0xA -- declined.  (once) ]' exactly once in each width's own
 333:      session: lb/lh/sb/sh said, hi said), and its CHANNEL -- fatal(), which prints when the guest free-runs, where a
 334:      debug() would not (free said)
 335:   6. the ACCEPT SET shrunk at any single cell (the de-escalation clause: the deleted exit(1) used to announce this),
 336:      at BOTH ends of the window:  the FIRST slot +0x00 -- acc8 near (8R, anchored to CRIME_REV's 0x11, which the
 337:      prelude writes; grok, closing pass: `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0' passed the 85-row version, whose rows sat at
 338:      +0x30 -- an interior slot, not an end), with devsig the 4R-low cell there and `rev pc ctl' the 4R-high one (its
 339:      redirect needs the access ACCEPTED; `devsig hi' reads 0 either way -- grok, codex, second closing pass, correcting
 340:      this sentence, which credited devsig hi); CRIME_DOG +0x30 -- acc8 st+ld
 341:      (8W, 8R), acc4 rhi, acc4 rlo, acc4 wlo, acc4 whi; the last slot +0x278 -- top8 st+ld, top4 rhi, top4 rlo, top4 w.  Those rows are ROUND TRIPS and cannot see an
 342:      accept arm that re-maps its register index (kimi-k3:cloud, F2: `d->reg[(relative_addr & 0x3f) / 8]' passed the
 343:      71-row version), so one row is ANCHORED: `top8 anchor' reads bank-1 control at +0x210, a value the PRELUDE writes
 344:      by bank index (1 for -M 64), not by the decode under test; `top4 anchor' reads its low half with a 4R.  The 4R
 345:      of its HIGH half answers 0 accepted or refused, so that cell is pinned by the ANNOUNCE instead (Opus closing:
 346:      `len == 4 && relative_addr != 0x210' passed the 85-row version): every accept cell the top session touches
 347:      before its one refusal is pinned the same way                                                      top said
 348:      *** It pins the model's CURRENT bank layout, which the
 349:      filed `crimebankorder' disputes; a round that re-orders the banks owes this row's re-derivation. ***
 350:   7. a refused store that LANDS: CRIME_DOG seeded by an accepted sd survives a refused sb and sh     sb keeps, sh keeps;
 351:      and at the far block, a refused sb +0x27f and sh +0x27c leave +0x278 reading 0 (Opus, F2 and closing: a store
 352:      landing only >= 0x200, of either width)                                                            hi keeps
 353:      and in the middle of the window (+0x1ff / +0x1fc, every address bit 0-8 set)                       mid keeps
 354:      and at INTMASK, read back by an accepted ld (kimi CLI, second closing pass: a landing that ORs into INTMASK kept
 355:      bit 28, so imask ctl could not see it)                                                             imask keeps
 356:   8. state carried ACROSS calls: a declined read after accepted reads of a nonzero register still answers 0 (an
 357:      `odata' made static), and an accepted access between two refusals does not re-arm the announce    warm v0,
 358:      warm once  (the codex seat, F2) -- closed over every place an accepted access can go after codex found the
 359:      4-byte arm's re-arm open on the closing pass: 8W, 8R, 4W and 4R in both lanes, and an accepted access into each
 360:      switch arm AND EACH BRANCH an accepted access can take and come back from -- CONTROL 0x0201 (the DOG_ENABLE and
 361:      unimplemented-control branches; codex, second closing pass: `d->warned = 0;' inside DOG_ENABLE passed), the REV
 362:      read from the PROM-probe window (the redirect branch), the default arm read and write -- each between two
 363:      refusals                                                                               rearm once, rearm surv
 364:      and in the middle block (an accepted sd between two refusals there)                                mid once
 365:   9. the ANSWER at each named label: a refused read at CRIME_REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG and TIME answers 0
 366:      (codex, closing pass: `if (relative_addr == CRIME_REV) odata = 1;' passed the 85-row version -- the REV rows
 367:      checked only the pc)          lb/lh rev v0, ctl v0, istat/imask lb/lh v0, dog v0, dog lh v0, time lb/lh v0
 368:      every one at 2 bytes (lh sees every value lb sees) and over a register whose LOW BYTES ARE NONZERO, so a decline
 369:      that answers the register shows (kimi CLI, second closing pass: INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG held 0 in their low
 370:      bytes and `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' there passed; codex: `odata = 0x100' at DOG passed the lb-only row)
 371:      -- REV 0x11, CONTROL 0x100, TIME 66-990 (low bytes); INTSTAT/INTMASK 0x0101000010000001 and DOG 0x0123..ef,
 372:      nonzero in the TOP lanes a label's refused read names as well (Opus, second closing pass: a decline answering
 373:      the ADDRESSED lanes of the register passed the 0x10000001 seed 121/121)
 374:      and in the far block and the middle block over a NONZERO slot, at both widths (Opus closing: a refused read
 375:      >= 0x200 answering the register; Opus, second closing pass: the 2-byte cell there)
 376:                                                                    top rd v0, top rd2 v0, mid lb v0, mid lh v0
 377:   10. the decline HOLDS WHILE AN INTERRUPT IS LATCHED: HARDINT set by the guest through MACE and the CRIME line
 378:       asserted, a refused sh 0x0800 at CONTROL still does not stop the CPUs (the Opus closing seat: `if
 379:       (!d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8])' -- a field the tail reads -- and `if (!d->prev_asserted)' passed the 85-row
 380:       version)                                        gate run, with gate hardint and gate line as its controls
 381: 
 382: DISCLOSED, not rowed:
 383:   * `goto crime_declined' -> `return 1' is EQUIVALENT on every channel: memory_rw pre-zeroes a device read buffer
 384:     (#95, src/cpus/memory_rw.c -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)' then `memset(data, 0, len);', immediately above
 385:     `paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr;'), and on a declined access nothing between the entry and the tail writes
 386:     HARDINT or SOFTINT, so the tail's restore and recompute are identities.  Measured 40/40 (the then 40-row test) in kill table 1.
 387:   * a fall-through confined to the two label cells whose arms have NO body for that access, both EQUIVALENT:
 388:       CRIME_DOG (0x30)       `No warning.' -- the arm is `break;'
 389:       CRIME_CONTROL, reads   the arm acts only on MEM_WRITE
 390:     Each measured as a survivor in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through).
 391:   * ACCEPT cells at offsets other than +0x00, +0x30, +0x210, +0x1f8 and +0x278: an accept arm narrowed by an address
 392:     strictly INSIDE
 393:     the window (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0x100', kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.
 394:   * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
 395:     label) -- a refused store that lands only at
 396:     +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
 397:     +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
 398:     offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
 399:     keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
 400:     kind (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8
 401:     carried by no refusal at all).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
 402:     OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
 403:     which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
 404:     (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
 405:   * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
 406:     every access, so no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted (the CONTROL arm's effects are
 407:     rowed separately as sw08 halts / sw08 msg ctl).  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc ctl' (its redirect
 408:     needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
 409:   * a decline answering the ADDRESSED LANES of the register at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME is EQUIVALENT:
 410:     a refused read at a label reads its bytes 0-1, which the model holds at 0 there (the prelude rewrites REV = 0x11
 411:     and CONTROL = 0x100 on every access; TIME is kept to 32 bits).  At INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG the seeds make those
 412:     lanes nonzero, so the same edit shows (Opus, second closing pass).
 413:   * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
 414:     default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
 415:   * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
 416:     CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
 417:   * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
 418:     (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
 419:   * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
 420:     process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
 421:     recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
 422:   * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
 423:     not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
 424:   * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).
 425: 
 426: ```

 753: ## THE MUTANTS OF TABLES 5 AND 6, verbatim (so each named mutant's EDIT is visible, not just its name)
 754: 
 755: Each entry is (find-text, replacement) on `dev_sgi_ip32.c`; `g(...)` puts its argument in place of the decline arm's
 756: `goto crime_declined;`.
 757: 
 758: ```python
 759: M.update({
 760:     # codex: the latch bit keyed on ONE label (the define is used by both the test and the set)
 761:     "c2_lbl_imask": [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK))")],
 762:     "c2_lbl_istat": [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT))")],
 763:     "c2_lbl_ctl":   [(DEFINE, "#define\tCRIME_W_LEN\t\t(1 << (relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL))")],
 764:     # codex: a halfword-only nonzero answer at DOG; kimi CLI: a decline answering THE REGISTER at a label
 765:     "c2_dog100":    [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_DOG) odata = 0x100; goto crime_declined;"))],
 766:     "c2_reg_imask": [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
 767:     "c2_reg_istat": [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
 768:     "c2_reg_dog":   [(GOTO, g("if (relative_addr == CRIME_DOG) odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]; goto crime_declined;"))],
 769:     # kimi CLI: a landing that ORs into INTMASK
 770:     "c2_land_or":   [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && relative_addr == CRIME_INTMASK) "
 771:                               "d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / 8] |= idata; goto crime_declined;"))],
 772:     # codex: re-arm inside CONTROL's DOG_ENABLE branch; and the same inside the other branches an accepted access takes
 773:     "c2_rearm_dog": [(DOGBR, DOGBR + "\t\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n")],
 774:     "c2_rearm_txt": [(TXTBR, "\t\t\tif (idata != 0 && ((d->warned = 0) == 0))\n\t\t\t\tfatal(\"[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented \"\n")],
 775:     "c2_rearm_rev": [(REVIF, REVIF + "\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n")],
 776:     # glm: a pre-set in a switch arm -- the INTMASK/INTSTAT arm (PREDICTED DETECTED) and the REV arm (the open member)
 777:     "c2_pre_irq":   [(IRQARM, IRQARM.replace("\t\tcrime_interrupt_reassert(d);\n",
 778:                                              "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n\t\tcrime_interrupt_reassert(d);\n"))],
 779:     "c2_pre_rev":   [(REVIF, "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + REVIF)],
 780:     # kimi-k3:cloud: decline effects keyed on an ADDRESS BIT no refusal carried (bit 7, bit 8)
 781:     "c2_bit7_ans":  [(GOTO, g("odata = relative_addr & 0x80; goto crime_declined;"))],
 782:     "c2_bit8_land": [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && (relative_addr & 0x100)) d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata; "
 783:                               "goto crime_declined;"))],
 784:     "c2_bit7_quiet": [(B.LATCH_TEST, "\t\tif (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN) && !(relative_addr & 0x80)) {")],
 785:     "c2_bit8_ft":   [(GOTO, g("if (!(relative_addr & 0x100)) goto crime_declined;"))],
 786:     "c2_bit8_rearm": [(ARM8, ARM8.replace("\tif (len == 8) {\n", "\tif (len == 8) {\n\t\tif (relative_addr & 0x100) d->warned = 0;\n", 1))],
 787:     "c2_bit8_latch": [(B.LATCH_SET, "\t\t\td->warned |= (relative_addr & 0x100) ? 0 : CRIME_W_LEN;\n")],
 788: })
 789: 
 790: LANE = "odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7))); goto crime_declined;"
 791: 
 792: M = dict(E.M)
 793: _p = os.path.join(HERE, "mutants_close2_opus_b3.py")
 794: _ns = {}
 795: exec(io.open(_p, encoding="utf-8").read().split("name, path = sys.argv")[0], _ns)
 796: M.update({"q_" + k: v for k, v in _ns["M"].items() if k != "base"})
 797: 
 798: 
 799: def lane(label):
 800:     return [(GOTO, g("if (writeflag == MEM_READ && relative_addr == %s) %s" % (label, LANE)))]
 801: 
 802: 
 803: M.update({
 804:     # the Opus seat's istat_lane, at the other labels: INTMASK and DOG are seeded and should show;
 805:     # REV, CONTROL and TIME hold 0 in the lanes a label's refused read names -- PREDICTED EQUIVALENT, disclosed
 806:     "l_imask": lane("CRIME_INTMASK"),
 807:     "l_dog":   lane("CRIME_DOG"),
 808:     "l_rev":   lane("CRIME_REV"),
 809:     "l_ctl":   lane("CRIME_CONTROL"),
 810:     "l_time":  lane("CRIME_TIME"),
 811: })

 209: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
 210:     """one emulator, read to the pty's EOF after `quit'.  Returns (text, clean): clean = EOF reached AND exit 0."""
 211:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 212:     if pid == 0:
 213:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64", "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
 214:         os._exit(127)
 215:     buf = ""
 216: 
 217:     def rd(t):
 218:         nonlocal buf
 219:         end = time.time() + t
 220:         while time.time() < end:
 221:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 222:             if not r:
 223:                 continue
 224:             try:
 225:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 226:             except OSError:
 227:                 return False
 228:             if not d:
 229:                 return False
 230:             buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 231:         return True
 232: 
 233:     rd(settle)
 234:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 235:         try:
 236:             if c == "<RUN>":        # free-run: `continue', let the guest run, then break back in with CTRL-C
 237:                 os.write(fd, b"continue\n")
 238:                 ok = rd(2.0)
 239:                 os.write(fd, b"\x03")
 240:                 ok = ok and rd(1.5)
 241:             else:
 242:                 os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 243:                 ok = rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25)
 244:         except OSError:
 245:             break
 246:         if not ok:
 247:             break
 248:     eof = True                      # a process already gone before `quit' has closed the pty; its status says how
 249:     try:
 250:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
 251:         eof = not rd(10.0)          # to EOF, so anything printed on the way out is in the transcript
 252:     except OSError:
 253:         pass
 254:     if not eof:
 255:         try:
 256:             os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)    # a session that never reached EOF is ended, never waited on forever
 257:         except OSError:
 258:             pass
 259:     try:
 260:         os.close(fd)
 261:     except OSError:
 262:         pass

 264:     try:

 267:     except OSError:
 268:         pass

 469: # ---- the INTSTAT / INTMASK labels: their arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), which is NOT idempotent -- ----
 470: #  INTMASK = bit 28 and SOFTINT = bit 28 are stored with 8-byte writes; SOFTINT's store goes to the default arm and does
 471: #  NOT reassert, so the CPU's IP2 (cause bit 0x400) stays low until something reasserts.  A refused access at the label
 472: #  must leave it low; the accepted `lw' that follows is the CONTROL that the arm reasserts at once.  (Measured by the
 473: #  pass-2 Opus seat, opus_pass2_20260913/scripts/crime_p2_escape.py E1/E2: a fall-through at the label sets IP2.)
 474: #  BIT carries bit 0 as well (the kimi CLI seat, second closing pass): a refused read answers the low bytes of whatever
 475: #  the decline hands the tail, so with a register whose low bytes were 0 a decline that answered THE REGISTER
 476: #  (`odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' at INTSTAT or INTMASK) read back 0 and passed.  AND in its TOP lanes (the Opus
 477: #  second-closing seat, measured 121/121 on the 0x10000001 seed): a decline that answers the bytes the ADDRESS names --
 478: #  `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))', the lane the model's own 4-byte read
 479: #  path uses -- reads bytes 0-1 at the label, which the 0x10000001 seed left 0.  So the seed is nonzero in byte 0, byte 1
 480: #  and the low byte; INTSTAT = SOFTINT | HARDINT and INTMASK both carry it; the reassert still sees bit 28.
 481: BIT = 0x0101000010000001
 482: 
 483: 
 484: def nth(t, reg, n):
 485:     ms = re.findall(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 486:     return int(ms[n], 16) if len(ms) > n else None
 487: 
 488: 
 489: def ip2(v):
 490:     return "absent" if v is None else ("set" if v & 0x400 else "clear")
 491: 
 492: 
 493: for lbl, off in (("imask", 0x18), ("istat", 0x10)):
 494:     A = Addr(CODE)
 495:     c = []
 496:     x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
 497:     x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
 498:     c += ["print cause"]
 499:     x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + off, "a0"); c += x
 500:     c += ["print cause"]
 501:     x, _ = st(A, "sb", CRIME + off, 0xff); c += x
 502:     c += ["print cause"]
 503:     x, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + off, "a1"); c += x
 504:     c += ["print cause"]
 505:     x, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + off, 0xffff); c += x
 506:     c += ["print cause"]
 507:     x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + off, "v0"); c += x
 508:     c += ["print cause", "print a0", "print a1"]
 509:     #  INTMASK read back by an accepted ld after the refused stores (kimi CLI, second closing pass: a landing that ORs
 510:     #  into INTMASK keeps bit 28, so the reassert rows cannot see it), then a follow-up refusal at ANOTHER label (codex:
 511:     #  a latch keyed on this one label is invisible to a session that refuses only here)
 512:     x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x18, "s2"); c += x
 513:     x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "t1"); c += x
 514:     c += ["print s2"]
 515:     t, clean = session(c)
 516:     row("%s pre" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 0)), "clear", "IP2 low after SOFTINT's default-arm store")
 517:     row("%s lb" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 1)), "clear", "refused lb +0x%x: no reassert" % off)
 518:     row("%s sb" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 2)), "clear", "refused sb +0x%x: no reassert" % off)
 519:     row("%s lh" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 3)), "clear", "refused lh +0x%x: no reassert" % off)
 520:     row("%s sh" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 4)), "clear", "refused sh +0x%x: no reassert" % off)
 521:     row("%s ctl" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 5)), "set", "CONTROL: accepted lw +0x%x reasserts" % off)
 522:     row("%s once" % lbl, count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "4 refusals at +0x%x, then +0x07: one notice" % off)
 523:     row("%s lb v0" % lbl, pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused lb +0x%x answers 0 (register 0x0101..01)" % off)
 524:     row("%s lh v0" % lbl, pr(t, "a1"), "0x0", "refused lh +0x%x answers 0 (register 0x0101..01)" % off)
 525:     if lbl == "imask":
 526:         row("imask keeps", pr(t, "s2"), "0x%x" % BIT, "refused sb/sh +0x18 leave INTMASK as stored (ld +0x18)")
 527: 
 528: # ---- THE DECLINE HOLDS WHILE AN INTERRUPT IS LATCHED (the Opus closing seat: `if (!d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8]) goto
 529: #  crime_declined;' passed the 85-row version -- every refusal in it ran with HARDINT == 0, and the tail the goto jumps
 530: #  to READS HARDINT, so the field is a DEPENDS-ON operand; `if (!d->prev_asserted)' passed too).  The guest latches a
 531: #  hardware interrupt by two MACE stores (ISA interrupt status, then mask -> crime_interrupt_assert -> HARDINT), and
 532: #  -- INDEPENDENTLY, not as a consequence (grok, second closing pass: HARDINT's bit is not in the mask used here) --
 533: #  asserts the CRIME line (INTMASK and SOFTINT = BIT, reasserted by an accepted lw), then a refused sh 0x0800 at
 534: #  CRIME_CONTROL: falling into the switch there would stop every CPU.  Both states are rowed as controls: HARDINT reads
 535: #  nonzero, and IP2 is set.
 536: MACE = 0xbf310000                                   # KSEG1 of the `mace addr=0x1f310000' device (dev_sgi_ip32.c)
 537: A = Addr(CODE)
 538: c = []
 539: for off, val in ((0x10, 0x01), (0x18, 0x01)):
 540:     x, _ = st(A, "sd", MACE + off, val); c += x
 541: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
 542: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
 543: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x18, "a0"); c += x
 544: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x28, "s2"); c += x
 545: c += ["print s2", "print cause"]
 546: x, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x08, 0x0800); c += x
 547: t, clean = session(c + roundtrip(A))
 548: hint = nth(t, "s2", 0)
 549: row("gate hardint", "set" if hint else ("absent" if hint is None else "clear"), "set",
 550:     "CONTROL: two MACE stores latch HARDINT (raw %s)" % hint)
 551: row("gate line", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 0)), "set", "CONTROL: the CRIME line is asserted")
 552: row("gate run", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "refused sh 0x800 +0x08 with an irq latched: CPUs run")
 553: 
 554: # ---- the TIME label: an accepted read returns the value of the PREVIOUS update, then updates -- so a refused access
 555: #  that reached crime_update_crime_time() would show in the NEXT read.  The first `ld' latches last_microseconds; on the
 556: #  fixed build the only other update before the next `ld' is the device tick's FIRST firing -- ticks_till_next starts at
 557: #  0, so it fires right after the session's first `ld' (the Opus closing seat; this said "nothing else updates TIME"
 558: #  until then), and it adds the few microseconds since the latch: the 66-990 the rows print (measured across 52 cells
 559: #  against SMALL = 6.6e6).  Later ticks are 2^14 cycles apart and a session steps a few dozen instructions; a fall-through at 0x38
 560: #  makes it the wall-clock interval of the intervening debugger commands, ~1e8.  The third `ld' is the CONTROL that the
 561: #  channel moves.  (Opus pass 2, E3; the tick-spacing wording corrected by the kimi CLI seat on the F2 pass.)
 562: #  ASSUMPTION, disclosed: after that FIRST tick, no further tick falls between the first `ld' and the `ld' that samples
 563: #  s1 (codex, closing pass: the window runs THROUGH the sampling read, not only to the refused access; deepseek and
 564: #  codex, second closing pass: this sentence said "no tick boundary" while the one above names the first tick).  The IRQ rows carry the same assumption:
 565: #  dev_crime_tick also calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), so a tick inside a session could raise IP2 on the fixed build
 566: #  (kimi CLI, closing pass) -- a false red, never a false green; measured stable so far.
 567: SMALL = 66 * 100000
 568: 
 569: 
 570: def mag(v):
 571:     return "absent" if v is None else ("small" if v < SMALL else "large")
 572: 
 573: 
 574: for op in ("lb", "sb", "lh", "sh"):
 575:     A = Addr(CODE)
 576:     c = []
 577:     x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s0"); c += x
 578:     if op in ("lb", "lh"):
 579:         x, _ = ld(A, op, CRIME + 0x38, "v0"); c += x
 580:     else:
 581:         x, _ = st(A, op, CRIME + 0x38, 0xff if op == "sb" else 0xffff); c += x
 582:     x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s1"); c += x
 583:     x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s2"); c += x
 584:     if op == "lb":                                  # a second refusal: the TIME-first latch cell
 585:         x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v1"); c += x
 586:     t, clean = session(c + ["print s1", "print s2", "print v0"])
 587:     s1, s2 = nth(t, "s1", 0), nth(t, "s2", 0)
 588:     row("time %s" % op, mag(s1), "small", "after refused %s +0x38 (raw %s)" % (op, s1))
 589:     row("time %s ctl" % op, mag(s2), "large", "CONTROL: accepted ld moved TIME (raw %s)" % s2)
 590:     if op in ("lb", "lh"):
 591:         row("time %s v0" % op, pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused %s +0x38 answers 0" % op)
 592:     if op == "lb":
 593:         row("time once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "first refusal at CRIME_TIME, then +0x07: one notice")
 594: 
 595: # ---- a refused store leaves the register it names untouched (CRIME_DOG seeded by an accepted sd) ----
 596: A = Addr(CODE)
 597: c = []
 598: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x30, 0x0123456789abcdef); c += x
 599: x, _ = st(A, "sb", CRIME + 0x37, 0xa5); c += x
 600: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x30, "a0"); c += x
 601: x, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x30, 0x5a5a); c += x
 602: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x30, "a1"); c += x
 603: #  the codex seat, F2: every other refused read runs in a COLD session, so an `odata' that survived from the previous
 604: #  call (`static uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;') answered 0 there by luck.  Here a refused lbu follows accepted reads
 605: #  of a nonzero register; and the announce count across refused-accepted-refused catches a latch re-armed by an
 606: #  accepted access (`d->warned = 0;' in an accept arm), which `latch x5' -- no accepted access -- cannot.
 607: x, _ = ld(A, "lbu", CRIME + 0x07, "v0"); c += x
 608: t, clean = session(c + ["print a0", "print a1", "print v0"])
 609: row("sb keeps", pr(t, "a0"), "0x123456789abcdef", "refused sb +0x37 leaves CRIME_DOG as seeded")
 610: row("sh keeps", pr(t, "a1"), "0x123456789abcdef", "refused sh +0x30 leaves CRIME_DOG as seeded")
 611: row("warm v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lbu AFTER accepted reads of 0x0123..: still 0")
 612: row("warm once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "refused, accepted, refused: still one announce")
 613: 
 614: #  RE-ARM, closed over every place an accepted access can go (codex, closing pass: `d->warned = 0;' in the 4-byte arm
 615: #  passed the 85-row version -- `warm once' interleaves only 8-byte accesses; the Opus seat measured it 85/85).  One
 616: #  refusal latches; then each ACCEPTED kind -- 8W, 8R, 4W and 4R in both lanes, and an accepted access into each switch
 617: #  arm (DOG, TIME, INTMASK, INTSTAT, REV, CONTROL, the default arm low and in the far block) -- is followed by a refusal.
 618: #  A re-arm anywhere an accepted access reaches shows as a second notice.  (A re-arm in the common tail, which refusals
 619: #  also reach, shows in latch x5.)
 620: #  The second closing pass (codex) found the arm's BRANCHES were not all reached: an accepted CONTROL write carried only
 621: #  0, 1 or 0x800, so a re-arm inside the DOG_ENABLE branch passed.  So the CONTROL write here is 0x0201 (the DOG branch
 622: #  and the unimplemented-control branch), the REV read runs from the PROM-probe window (its pc-redirect branch), and the
 623: #  default arm takes a 4-byte write too.  The HARD_RESET branch cannot be followed by anything (it stops every CPU):
 624: #  a re-arm there is DISCLOSED.
 625: A = Addr(CODE)
 626: c = ["ra=0x%x" % RA]
 627: x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v0"); c += x
 628: KINDS = (("sd", 0x30), ("ld", 0x30), ("sw", 0x34), ("lw", 0x34), ("sw", 0x30), ("lw", 0x30),
 629:          ("ld", 0x38), ("lw", 0x18), ("lw", 0x10), ("lw", 0x00), ("lwP", 0x00), ("sw", 0x08), ("lw", 0x04),
 630:          ("sw", 0x04), ("sd", 0x278), ("ld", 0x278))
 631: for op, off in KINDS:
 632:     if op == "lwP":                                 # from pc 0xbfc05180: the REV arm's redirect branch (pc = ra)
 633:         x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + off, "a0", at=PROBE_PC)
 634:     elif op[0] == "s":
 635:         x, _ = st(A, op, CRIME + off, 0x0201 if off == 0x08 else 0)
 636:     else:
 637:         x, _ = ld(A, op, CRIME + off, "a0")
 638:     c += x
 639:     x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x06, "v0"); c += x
 640: t, clean = session(c + roundtrip(A))
 641: row("rearm once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "%d refusals, %d accepted kinds between: one notice"
 642:     % (len(KINDS) + 1, len(KINDS)))
 643: row("rearm surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "the whole interleave completes (a RAM trip)")
 644: 
 645: # ---- ACCEPT cells at the window's LAST 8-byte slot (+0x278; no case label, no prelude write) -- the far bound ----
 646: A = Addr(CODE)
 647: c = []
 648: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x210, "s2"); c += x
 649: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x214, "s1"); c += x      # 4R of the anchored slot's low half
 650: #  4R of its HIGH half (the Opus closing seat: `len == 4 && relative_addr != 0x210' passed the 85-row version).  Its
 651: #  value is 0 accepted or refused -- the prelude rewrites the slot on every access -- so the row is the ANNOUNCE: a
 652: #  refused accept cell here takes the session's one notice, and `top said' below reads 0.
 653: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x210, "t1"); c += x
 654: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x278, 0x0102030405060708); c += x
 655: x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x27b, "v0"); c += x      # a REFUSED read while the far slot is nonzero (Opus: rd_hi)
 656: x, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x27a, "s3"); c += x      # and a refused 2-byte read there (Opus, second closing pass)
 657: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x278, "a0"); c += x
 658: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x278, "a1"); c += x
 659: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x27c, "a2"); c += x
 660: x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x27c, 0x0a0b0c0d); c += x
 661: x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x278, 0x11112222); c += x
 662: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x278, "s0"); c += x
 663: t, clean = session(c + ["print s2", "print a0", "print a1", "print a2", "print s0", "print s1", "print v0",
 664:                         "print s3"])
 665: row("top8 anchor", pr(t, "s2"), "0x1", "8R +0x210: bank-1 ctrl the PRELUDE wrote (-M 64)")
 666: row("top4 anchor", pr(t, "s1"), "0x1", "4R +0x214: the anchored slot's low half")
 667: row("top rd v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb +0x27b over a nonzero slot answers 0")
 668: row("top rd2 v0", pr(t, "s3"), "0x0", "refused lh +0x27a over a nonzero slot answers 0")
 669: row("top said", said(t, 1, 0x27b, clean), 1, "lb +0x27b is the first refusal: every access before it accepted")
 670: row("top8 st+ld", pr(t, "a0"), "0x102030405060708", "8W then 8R at +0x278")
 671: row("top4 rhi", pr(t, "a1"), "0x1020304", "4R high half at +0x278")
 672: row("top4 rlo", pr(t, "a2"), "0x5060708", "4R low half at +0x27c")
 673: row("top4 w", pr(t, "s0"), "0x111122220a0b0c0d", "4W both halves at +0x278/+0x27c")
 674: 
 675: # ---- a refused width at the window's LAST byte: declined, announced, default arm not reached ----
 676: A = Addr(CODE)
 677: x, here = st(A, "sb", CRIME + 0x27f, 0xff)
 678: w, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x27c, 0xffff)      # a refused 2-byte store in the far block too (Opus closing: wst_mc2)
 679: y, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x278, "a0")        # the refused stores' register, read back by an ACCEPTED ld
 680: z, _ = st(A, "sb", CRIME + 0x27e, 0xee)        # follow-up refusals: one in the high block (a latch keyed on >= 0x40)
 681: z2, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v0")        #   and one low (a latch whose bit depends on the block, `lt_blk')
 682: t, clean = session(x + AFTER + roundtrip(A) + w + y + ["print a0"] + z + z2)
 683: row("hi said", said(t, 1, 0x27f, clean), 1, "sb +0x27f: own announce, once")
 684: row("hi nodflt", dflt(t, clean, 0x27f, True), 0, "switch default arm NOT reached at 0x27f")
 685: row("hi surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "host survives, then a RAM trip")
 686: row("hi keeps", pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused sb +0x27f, sh +0x27c do not land (ld +0x278)")
 687: row("hi once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "first refusal +0x27f, then 0x27c/0x27e/0x07: one notice")
 688: 
 689: # ---- THE MIDDLE OF THE WINDOW, where EVERY address bit 0-8 is set (kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass: no refused access
 690: #  set bit 7 or bit 8, so a decline effect keyed on `relative_addr & 0x80' -- `odata = relative_addr & 0x80' -- passed).
 691: #  Together with the low cells (bits clear) and the far block (bit 9), every address bit is now carried both ways by a
 692: #  refused access of each kind: the ANSWER (1R, 2R), a LANDING (1W, 2W, read back by an accepted ld), the NOTICE (its
 693: #  text here, and one count across the session), the DEFAULT ARM not reached, and a RE-ARM by an accepted access in
 694: #  this block (the sd between the refusals).  +0x1f8 is inside DEV_CRIME_LENGTH (0x280) and reaches the default arm.
 695: A = Addr(CODE)
 696: c = []
 697: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x1f8, 0x0123456789abcdef); c += x
 698: x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x1ff, "v0"); c += x
 699: x, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x1fe, "a1"); c += x
 700: x, _ = st(A, "sb", CRIME + 0x1ff, 0xff); c += x
 701: x, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x1fc, 0xffff); c += x
 702: x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x1f8, "a0"); c += x
 703: x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x1f8, 0x0123456789abcdef); c += x
 704: x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x1ff, "t1"); c += x
 705: t, clean = session(c + ["print v0", "print a1", "print a0"] + roundtrip(A))
 706: mid_dflt = (dflt(t, clean, 0x1ff, False) + dflt(t, clean, 0x1fe, False) + dflt(t, clean, 0x1ff, True) +
 707:             dflt(t, clean, 0x1fc, True)) if clean else "unclean"
 708: row("mid said", said(t, 1, 0x1ff, clean), 1, "lb +0x1ff (bits 0-8 set): own announce, once")
 709: row("mid nodflt", mid_dflt, 0, "switch default arm NOT reached at 0x1ff/0x1fe/0x1fc")
 710: row("mid lb v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb +0x1ff over a seeded slot answers 0")
 711: row("mid lh v0", pr(t, "a1"), "0x0", "refused lh +0x1fe over a seeded slot answers 0")
 712: row("mid keeps", pr(t, "a0"), "0x123456789abcdef", "refused sb +0x1ff, sh +0x1fc do not land (ld +0x1f8)")
 713: row("mid once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "5 refusals with an accepted sd between: one notice")
 714: row("mid surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "the session completes (a RAM trip)")
 715: 
 716: # ---- the latch, first refusal at CRIME_DOG (the one named label with no session whose FIRST refusal is there) ----
 717: #  CRIME_DOG is SEEDED first (kimi CLI, second closing pass: with DOG at 0 a decline answering the register read 0), and
 718: #  read refused at BOTH widths (codex: `odata = 0x100' at DOG left the lb's low byte 0 and passed).
 719: A = Addr(CODE)
 720: s, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x30, 0x0123456789abcdef)
 721: x, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x30, "v0")
 722: y, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v1")
 723: z, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x08, "a0")        # a refused read at CRIME_CONTROL (codex, closing pass: answers at labels)
 724: w, _ = ld(A, "lh", CRIME + 0x30, "t1")
 725: t, clean = session(s + x + y + z + w + ["print v0", "print a0", "print t1"])
 726: row("dog once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "first refusal at CRIME_DOG, then +0x07: one notice")
 727: row("dog v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "refused lb at CRIME_DOG (seeded 0x0123..ef) answers 0")
 728: row("dog lh v0", pr(t, "t1"), "0x0", "refused lh at CRIME_DOG (seeded) answers 0")
 729: row("ctl v0", pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused lh at CRIME_CONTROL (holds 0x100) answers 0")
 730: 
 731: # ---- FREE-RUNNING: the announce is a fatal(), so it prints when the guest is NOT single-stepped (a debug() would not) ----
 732: A = Addr(CODE)
 733: here = A.take(2)
 734: c = ["at=0x%x" % (CRIME + 7), "v0=0x%x" % SENT,
 735:      "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here, enc("lb", "at", "v0")),
 736:      "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 4, OP_NOP),
 737:      "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 8, OP_B_SELF),
 738:      "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 12, OP_NOP),
 739:      "pc=0x%x" % here, "<RUN>", "print v0"]
 740: t, clean = session(c)
 741: ran = t.split("continue", 1)[1] if "continue" in t else ""
 742: row("free said", ran.count("unimplemented len=1 at 0x7 -- declined.  (once) ]"), 1, "free-running lb +7: announce printed")
 743: row("free v0", pr(t, "v0"), "0x0", "free-running lb +7: declined read answers 0")
 744: 
 745: # ---- the latch: five refused accesses, one session, exactly one announce ----
 746: A = Addr(CODE)
 747: c = []
 748: #  PROLOGUE (the Opus closing seat): accepted accesses BEFORE the first refusal -- a notice PRE-SILENCED by an accepted
 749: #  access (`d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;' in an accept arm, `pre4') shows as zero notices; and an accepted CONTROL write
 750: #  whose unmodelled bit prints the CONTROL text, so a future second latch on THAT message reusing bit 0 (`ctl_collide')
 751: #  shows the same way.  8-byte pre-sets show in warm once (sd first) and time once (ld first).
 752: for op, off, val in (("sw", 0x08, 0x01), ("sw", 0x34, 0x0badcafe)):
 753:     x, _ = st(A, op, CRIME + off, val); c += x
 754: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x34, "a0"); c += x
 755: #  and an accepted read of CRIME_REV (glm, second closing pass: no counted session reached the REV arm before its first
 756: #  refusal, so a pre-set there passed).  The other arms are reached first elsewhere: CONTROL here, INTMASK in the IRQ
 757: #  sessions' setup, DOG in warm once and the dog session, TIME in time once, the default arm here and in the top session.
 758: x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x00, "a1"); c += x

 760: #  sets bit 0 only for a 1-byte access or an odd address, so the lh at +0x06 would leave it clear and the lb after it
 761: #  would announce again.  With lb first, both shapes passed.
 762: for op, off in (("lh", 6), ("lb", 7), ("lbu", 0x3f)):
 763:     x, _ = ld(A, op, CRIME + off, "v0"); c += x
 764: for op, off, val in (("sb", 0x37, 0xab), ("sh", 0x36, 0xabcd)):

 767: row("latch x5", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "5 refused accesses -> one (once)")
 768: row("latch surv", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "host survives all five")
 769: 
 770: npass = sum(1 for _, ok in rows if ok)
 771: st_ = dict(rows)
 772: control = all(st_.get(n, False) for n in ("live", "devsig", "devsig hi", "dflt ctl", "rev pc ctl", "sw08 halts",
 773:                                           "gate hardint", "gate line",

  98: void crime_interrupt_reassert(struct crime_data *d)
  99: {
 100: 	uint64_t status =
 101: 		d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 102: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 103: 
 104: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = status;
 105: 
 106: 	status &= d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 107: 
 108: #if 0
 109: 	printf("CRIME SOFTINT=0x%08x HARDINT=0x%08x => 0x%08x, INTMASK=0x%08x\n",
 110: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 111: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 112: 		(uint32_t)status,
 113: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 114: #endif
 115: 	
 116: 	int asserted = !!status;
 117: 
 118: 	if (asserted && !d->prev_asserted)
 119: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 120: 	else if (!asserted && d->prev_asserted)
 121: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 122: 	
 123: 	d->prev_asserted = asserted;
 124: }

 127:  *  crime_interrupt_assert():
 128:  *  crime_interrupt_deassert():
 129:  */
 130: void crime_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 131: {
 132: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 133: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |= interrupt->line;
 134: 	//printf("CRIME asserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 135: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 136: }
 137: void crime_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 138: {
 139: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 140: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] &= ~interrupt->line;
 141: 	//printf("CRIME deasserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 142: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 143: }

 146: void crime_update_crime_time(struct crime_data* d)
 147: {
 148: 	struct timeval tv;
 149: 	
 150: 	gettimeofday(&tv, NULL);
 151: 
 152: 	uint64_t microseconds = tv.tv_sec * 1000000 + tv.tv_usec;
 153: 	if (d->last_microseconds == 0)
 154: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 155: 
 156: 	int64_t delta = microseconds - d->last_microseconds;
 157: 
 158: 	if (delta < 0) {
 159: 		fatal("[ crime_update_crime_time: host system time went backwards? ]\n");
 160: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 161: 		delta = 0;
 162: 	}
 163: 
 164: 	// The delta to add is 66 per microsecond.
 165: 	int64_t to_add = delta * 66;
 166: 
 167: 	if (to_add >= 1) {
 168: 		// NetBSD says CRIME_TIME_MASK = 0x0000ffffffffffffULL
 169: 		// but my O2 seems to use only the lower 32 bits as an
 170: 		// _unsigned_ value. (TODO: Double-check this again.)
 171: 		d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 172: 			(uint32_t)(d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] + to_add);
 173: 
 174: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 175: 	}
 176: }

 182:  *  Updates CRIME_TIME (at 66 MHz) and reassert CRIME interrupts.

 184: DEVICE_TICK(crime)
